# MARION-IA-USA Qwen3.8-27B Hermes Context Failure Fix Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-19  
**Priority:** High — Qwen3.8-27B currently cannot be used reliably by Hermes  
**Primary host:** MIAM-00111 / Proxmox VM103 / inference IP `10.0.20.161`  
**Control plane:** LLM Manager VM114 / `10.0.20.108`  
**Target model:** `cyankiwi/Qwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4`  
**Target served name:** `qwen3.8-27b`  
**Required Hermes context:** **70,000 tokens**  
**Desired concurrency:** 2–3 Hermes agents  
**Execution target:** 30–90 minutes; stop after 2 hours and preserve evidence if not fixed

---

## 1. Problem statement

The Telegram Hermes agent reports:

- `/model` successfully switches to `qwen3.8-27b`
- Provider is `marion`
- Hermes displays `Context: 70,000 tokens`
- A trivial message then immediately produces approximately:
  - `Context too large (~7,622 tokens) — compressing (1/3)...`
  - `Context length exceeded (...) Cannot compress further.`
  - `Session auto-reset`

DeepSeek and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B are working, so this task must focus narrowly on the Qwen3.8 request path and must **not destabilize the working models**.

The Qwen3.8 quantized model itself is not inherently limited to ~8K context. Its model config advertises `max_position_embeddings: 262144`. The intended MARION deployment intentionally caps it to **70,000** for Hermes and concurrency.

---

## 2. Leading hypothesis — test this FIRST

### H1 — Hermes is sending an illegal output-token cap to vLLM, then misclassifying the vLLM 400 as a prompt/context overflow

This symptom closely matches a current Hermes/vLLM bug family:

1. Hermes believes the context window is 70K.
2. The actual conversation is only ~7–8K tokens.
3. Hermes sends a request containing an excessive `max_tokens` / output cap.
4. vLLM rejects it because `input_tokens + max_tokens` cannot fit inside `max_model_len`.
5. Hermes classifies the vLLM 400 as a context overflow.
6. Hermes unnecessarily compresses the conversation.
7. The same illegal request is retried.
8. Hermes eventually says `Cannot compress further` and resets the session.

A public Hermes issue filed in September 2026 documents this exact failure mode with **Qwen3.8-27B INT4 on self-hosted vLLM**. The characteristic backend error is:

```text
max_tokens=... cannot be greater than max_model_len=max_total_tokens=...
Please request fewer output tokens
```

Do **not** assume this is the cause until the actual MARION logs show the same or equivalent response.

### Important distinction

`context_length` and `max_tokens` are not the same setting:

- `context_length = 70000` means the total request budget: system prompt + tool schemas + history + user input + generated output.
- `max_tokens` is only the maximum generated output for one response.

For Hermes agent work, keep the total context at **70,000**. Do **not** request 70,000 output tokens per turn.

---

## 3. Safety and rollback rules

Before changing anything:

1. Keep Qwen3.6 and DeepSeek deployments untouched.
2. Put only the MIAM-00111 / VM103 Qwen3.8 deployment into LLM Manager `MAINTENANCE` before restarting vLLM.
3. Do not reboot MIAM-00111.
4. Do not alter PCI passthrough, SR-IOV, RDMA, NIC, kernel, BIOS, or GPU binding.
5. Do not delete model caches.
6. Do not replace the known-good Qwen3.8 rollback preset.
7. Capture the current VM103 service and LLM Manager deployment before edits.
8. Back up the Hermes config/profile before changing it.
9. Prefer a configuration correction over a Hermes source patch.
10. If a Hermes source patch is required, make the smallest possible patch and save a diff/rollback copy.

Create a work directory such as:

```text
/home/jordatech/qwen38-hermes-fix-20260919-<HHMMSS>/
```

Save every diagnostic and changed file there.

---

## 4. Phase A — prove which layer is failing

**Timebox: 15–20 minutes. Do not make speculative changes before this phase is complete.**

There are four relevant layers:

```text
Hermes agent/profile
        ↓
LLM Manager / nginx / LiteLLM
        ↓
VM103 vLLM API
        ↓
Qwen3.8 model/runtime
```

We need to find the first layer that behaves incorrectly.

### A1. Capture Hermes version and active configuration

On the machine/container actually running `agent_stea004_entrepreneur`:

```bash
hermes --version 2>&1 || true
which hermes || true
python3 --version
```

Locate the active profile and gateway process rather than assuming paths:

```bash
ps auxww | grep -i '[h]ermes'
find ~/.hermes -maxdepth 4 -type f \( -name 'config.yaml' -o -name '*.yaml' -o -name '*.yml' \) -print 2>/dev/null
```

The existing environment has historically used a profile named:

```text
agent_stea004_entrepreneur
```

but verify the exact active profile/path from the running process.

Back up the relevant config before edits.

### A2. Inspect all context/output-token settings

Search active Hermes configuration and environment for:

```bash
grep -RniE 'context_length|max_tokens|max_output|compression|qwen3\.8|marion' ~/.hermes 2>/dev/null | head -300
env | grep -iE 'HERMES|MAX_TOKEN|CONTEXT' | sort
```

Record especially:

- `model.context_length`
- provider-specific `context_length`
- any `model.max_tokens`
- any `agent.max_tokens`
- any `HERMES_MAX_TOKENS`
- compression threshold settings
- the `marion` provider/model entry for `qwen3.8-27b`

Do not automatically equate an output cap with context length.

### A3. Capture the actual failing Hermes error

Reproduce once with a **new/fresh Hermes session** using `qwen3.8-27b` and a trivial prompt such as:

```text
Reply with only the word READY.
```

Immediately inspect Hermes gateway/agent logs. Search for:

```text
400
max_tokens
max_model_len
max_total_tokens
context length
context_overflow
compression
Cannot compress further
qwen3.8-27b
```

Example search:

```bash
grep -RniE 'max_tokens|max_model_len|max_total_tokens|cannot compress|context.*exceed|qwen3\.8' ~/.hermes/logs ~/.hermes 2>/dev/null | tail -300
```

**Highest-value evidence:** the raw HTTP/provider error returned by LLM Manager/vLLM before Hermes starts compressing.

### A4. Verify VM103 really serves 70K

From a machine that can reach VM103:

```bash
curl -sS http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1/models | python3 -m json.tool
```

Expected:

```text
id = qwen3.8-27b
max_model_len >= 70000
```

On VM103 also capture:

```bash
systemctl cat vllm
systemctl status vllm --no-pager
ps auxww | grep '[v]llm serve'
```

The actual running command must contain the intended:

```text
--max-model-len 70000
```

If direct `/v1/models` advertises less than 70K, skip to **Fix Path C** below.

### A5. Direct-backend short request

Send a tiny request directly to VM103 with a deliberately small output cap:

```json
{
  "model": "qwen3.8-27b",
  "messages": [{"role":"user","content":"Reply with READY only."}],
  "max_tokens": 64
}
```

Expected: HTTP 200.

If this fails, the problem is below LLM Manager/Hermes; investigate VM103/vLLM first.

### A6. Same request through LLM Manager

Send the same request using the normal LLM Manager API key and HTTPS endpoint, still with `max_tokens: 64`.

Expected: HTTP 200 routed to VM103.

If direct VM103 succeeds but LLM Manager fails, inspect the LLM Manager/LiteLLM request transformation before touching Hermes.

### A7. Inspect model metadata at both layers

Capture:

1. VM103 direct `/v1/models`
2. LLM Manager `/v1/models` using a valid agent key
3. LLM Manager registry row for `qwen3.8-27b`
4. LiteLLM model entry for `qwen3.8-27b`

Look specifically for accidental values such as:

```text
8192
32768
max_input_tokens
max_tokens
context_window
context_length
```

The intended value for the total model context is **70,000**.

---

## 5. Decision tree

Use the first matching path below.

---

# Fix Path A — oversized `max_tokens` / Hermes-vLLM parser bug

**Use this path if the raw error includes something equivalent to:**

```text
max_tokens=<N> cannot be greater than max_model_len=max_total_tokens=70000
```

or if a captured Hermes request clearly sends an absurdly large output budget.

This is the highest-probability path given the screenshot.

## A.1 Prefer removing the bad output-cap configuration

If the active Hermes version is sending a large `max_tokens` because one of these is explicitly configured:

```text
model.max_tokens
agent.max_tokens
HERMES_MAX_TOKENS
```

then remove/disable that oversized global setting **if it is not required by the working models**.

The goal is:

```text
context_length = 70000
output max_tokens = omitted OR a modest output cap
```

For a temporary targeted diagnostic, an output cap of **4096 or 8192** is reasonable. Do not confuse this with the 70K conversation context.

Restart only the Hermes gateway/process required to reload the config. Do not restart VM103 merely for a Hermes config change.

Re-test a fresh Qwen3.8 session.

### A.2 If current Hermes ignores configuration and still triggers the bug

Do **not** blindly upgrade the entire Hermes installation first.

Capture:

- exact Hermes version/commit
- whether the upstream vLLM error-parser fix is included
- local modifications

As of 2026-09-19, the public issue/fix for the specific vLLM error wording is still represented by open Hermes issue/PR work. If the installed Hermes build does not contain equivalent logic, apply the smallest local patch modeled on the upstream fix.

Patch only the error classification/parsing path so Hermes recognizes vLLM output-cap errors such as:

```text
max_model_len=max_total_tokens=70000
```

The patch needs to ensure:

1. the response is classified as an output-cap problem rather than prompt-too-long;
2. the model/context limit can be parsed from vLLM's error wording;
3. Hermes retries with a legal output cap rather than repeatedly compressing the prompt.

Before patching:

```bash
cp -a <hermes-source-file> <hermes-source-file>.pre-qwen38-fix-20260919
```

After patching, save:

```bash
git diff > qwen38-hermes-vllm-output-cap-fix.diff
```

Run any focused unit tests available for model metadata/output-cap parsing.

Then restart only Hermes/gateway and retest.

### A.3 Do not solve this by inflating VM103 context

Do not increase VM103 from 70K to 262K merely to hide an illegal Hermes output cap. That would consume more KV-cache capacity and work against the requirement for 2–3 concurrent coding agents.

---

# Fix Path B — Hermes stale/incorrect context metadata

Use this if:

- VM103 direct endpoint is genuinely 70K,
- LLM Manager is correct,
- no oversized `max_tokens` error exists,
- but Hermes internally behaves as though Qwen3.8 is 8K/16K/32K.

## B.1 Confirm what Hermes actually resolved

Use `/usage`, model-info output, startup logs, or equivalent to capture the resolved model window.

The `/model` UI displaying 70,000 is useful but not sufficient; confirm the actual runtime agent object/provider metadata also uses 70,000.

## B.2 Make the provider/model context explicit

For the MARION custom provider, explicitly set the Qwen3.8 entry to:

```yaml
context_length: 70000
```

Do not add a 70,000 `max_tokens` output setting.

If Hermes has a persistent discovered-context cache, remove/refresh **only the Qwen3.8/marion cache entry**, not unrelated model/provider state.

Restart the Hermes gateway and verify `/usage`/runtime metadata again.

---

# Fix Path C — VM103 is not actually serving 70K

Use this if direct:

```text
http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1/models
```

reports a lower `max_model_len` or the running vLLM command does not contain the intended 70K configuration.

## C.1 Preserve current service

Save:

```bash
systemctl cat vllm
systemctl status vllm --no-pager
ps auxww | grep '[v]llm serve'
```

Confirm the LLM Manager rollback preset exists.

## C.2 Restore the intended 70K production preset

Preferred Qwen3.8 configuration remains:

```text
model: cyankiwi/Qwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4
served model: qwen3.8-27b
max model len: 70000
TP: 2
DP: 3
PP: 1
EP: not applicable
prefix caching: enabled
chunked prefill: enabled
```

Use TP2/DP3 if it fits reliably. If 70K KV pressure prevents stable operation, use the already-planned TP3/DP2 fallback for two agents.

Do not reduce the context below 70K.

Restart only `vllm.service`, wait for `/v1/models`, and verify `max_model_len >= 70000` before returning LLM Manager desired state to `SERVING`.

---

# Fix Path D — LLM Manager/LiteLLM is mutating or mis-advertising Qwen3.8

Use this if:

- direct VM103 request succeeds,
- direct VM103 metadata is 70K,
- but the same request through LLM Manager fails or reports incorrect model metadata.

Inspect:

- model registry row
- alias `code`
- LiteLLM model configuration
- any `max_tokens`, `max_input_tokens`, `context_window`, or model-info overrides
- nginx routing only to confirm the expected request path

Correct the Qwen3.8 entry so LLM Manager forwards the request without inventing an output token budget and advertises/records the 70K total context accurately.

Do not alter the working `fast`/Qwen3.6 or DeepSeek entries.

---

## 6. Required acceptance tests after the fix

Do not call the issue fixed just because `/model` displays `70,000`.

### Test 1 — trivial fresh-session request

In a fresh Telegram/Hermes session:

1. select `qwen3.8-27b`;
2. verify it displays provider `marion` and 70,000 context;
3. send `Hello` or a one-second voice message;
4. receive a normal model response;
5. **no compression warning**;
6. **no auto-reset**.

This directly reproduces the previously failing behavior.

### Test 2 — tool-call smoke test

Ask Qwen3.8/Hermes to perform one benign tool call available to the agent.

Pass criteria:

- valid tool call
- tool result returned
- model continues afterward
- no context reset

### Test 3 — 10K–15K request/session

Build a controlled conversation/request above the ~7.6K point that previously failed.

Pass criteria:

- no false context-overflow recovery
- no `Cannot compress further`
- no reset

### Test 4 — 50K–60K context

Use a synthetic long-context request or accumulated session that reaches at least **50K tokens**, ideally ~60K, but remains under the 70K total budget with room for output.

Pass criteria:

- request accepted
- model returns useful output
- no vLLM OOM
- no Hermes false reset

Do not request 70K input plus a large output; total input + output must remain under the server context ceiling.

### Test 5 — concurrent coding-agent target

After single-agent correctness is proven:

- run 2 simultaneous Hermes agents through LLM Manager;
- if stable, run 3 simultaneous agents;
- use realistic moderate context first, then one long-context load test.

Minimum acceptable result: **2 concurrent agents**.  
Target result: **3 concurrent agents**.

Capture TTFT and obvious throughput degradation, but do not launch a long benchmark campaign.

---

## 7. LLM Manager cleanup after success

When accepted:

1. Save the fixed deployment as or update the preset:

```text
HERMES-QWEN38-70K-CODE-PRODUCTION
```

2. Preserve prior Qwen3.8 presets as rollback profiles.
3. Ensure `code` points to the fixed deployment.
4. Restore VM103 desired state to `SERVING`.
5. Ensure the registry has no stale healthy/routable Qwen3.8 row pointing to an old 25K/32K deployment.
6. Record the actual accepted:
   - vLLM command
   - TP/DP layout
   - context length
   - max-num-seqs
   - KV/cache configuration
   - Hermes version/commit
   - Hermes context configuration
   - whether a local Hermes patch was required
7. Save a concise incident note explaining the failure and fix.

---

## 8. Required final incident report

Write:

```text
QWEN38-HERMES-70K-CONTEXT-INCIDENT-20260919.md
```

It must contain:

### Root cause

State the proven cause, not the initial hypothesis.

### Evidence

Include:

- raw Hermes/provider error
- direct VM103 `/v1/models`
- manager `/v1/models`
- relevant Hermes config
- vLLM running command

### Fix

List exact files/settings changed.

### Rollback

Give exact rollback steps.

### Acceptance

Record PASS/FAIL for:

- fresh trivial Hermes request
- tool call
- >10K context
- 50K–60K context
- two concurrent agents
- three concurrent agents

### Remaining risks

Examples:

- local Hermes source patch may be overwritten by `hermes update`
- open upstream bug not yet merged/released
- 70K context reduces concurrency relative to 32K

---

## 9. Stop conditions

Stop and preserve evidence instead of making broad changes if any of these occur:

- Qwen3.6 or DeepSeek becomes unhealthy
- VM103 loses GPUs
- PCI/VFIO/SR-IOV errors appear
- a proposed fix requires Proxmox host reboot
- a proposed fix requires changing the working LLM Manager API-key architecture
- a Hermes full-version upgrade would overwrite unknown local modifications
- the issue cannot be isolated within 2 hours

If blocked, restore the previous VM103 preset and leave `code` disabled/unhealthy rather than advertising a broken model.

---

## 10. Recommended execution order

```text
0–10 min   Capture baseline + backups
10–20 min  Reproduce once and capture raw provider/vLLM error
20–30 min  Direct VM103 vs LLM Manager comparison
30–45 min  Apply the matching fix path
45–60 min  Fresh-session + >10K Hermes acceptance
60–90 min  50K–60K test + 2/3-agent concurrency + documentation
```

The task should not require changing model weights or re-downloading Qwen3.8 unless diagnostics prove the current model files are invalid.

---

## 11. Most likely successful outcome

Based on the observed screenshot, the highest-probability successful outcome is:

```text
VM103 Qwen3.8 itself is healthy at 70K.
LLM Manager routing is healthy.
Hermes sends or interprets an output-token limit incorrectly for the vLLM backend.
vLLM returns a 400 output-cap error.
Hermes mistakes that response for prompt/context overflow and enters futile compression.
Correcting/removing the oversized output cap or applying the narrow vLLM error-parser fix stops the false compression/reset loop.
```

Do not modify Qwen3.8 tensor/data parallelism unless the direct backend tests show an actual vLLM/context-capacity problem.

---

Use `terminal()` to invoke, `pty=true` for interactive mode, `background=true` for long tasks. |\n| **codex** | OpenAI Codex runs the same way — `terminal()` with either print mode (`-p`) or PTY mode. Cron jobs can trigger codex workflows. |\n| **opencode** | Provider-agnostic coding agent. Same invocation pattern via `terminal()`. Useful for multi-provider comparisons. |\n| **ruflo** | Ruflo is the orchestrator layer above Hermes. Hermes provides the terminal/execution environment; ruflo coordinates multi-agent swarms. |\n| **openharness** | OpenHarness provides the ohmo persona system. SOUL.md can be injected into Hermes via `--append-system-prompt-file`. |\n| **nomos** | NOMOS workflows can invoke Hermes via terminal. Use Hermes as the execution engine within NOMOS state machines. |\n| **armory** | Armory skills can be installed via `hermes skills install`. Install armory skill packages into `~/.hermes/skills/` or profile skill dirs. |\n| **caveman** | Token compression for Hermes output. Add to CLAUDE.md or agent system prompt for compressed responses. |\n| **hermes-agent** | This skill — the authoritative reference for configuring and extending Hermes itself. |\n\n## PITFALLS & GOTCHAS\n\n1. **Config changes require a fresh session** — Most config settings (`security.redact_secrets`, `approvals.mode`, toolsets) are snapshotted at import time. To apply changes, run `hermes config set KEY VAL` then start a new session (`/reset` in chat or `hermes` again in CLI). This is deliberate — it prevents an LLM from flipping the toggle on itself mid-task.\n\n2. **Secret redaction is OFF by default** — Tool output passes through unmodified. If you need API keys and secrets masked, run `hermes config set security.redact_secrets true` and restart. This does NOT affect `approvals.mode` — they are independent settings.\n\n3. **YOLO mode is NOT safe for secrets** — Setting `approvals.mode: off` or `--yolo` disables command approval but does NOT disable secret redaction. These are independent controls.\n\n4. **Toolset changes don't apply mid-conversation** — Enabling/disabling toolsets takes effect on `/reset` (new session) only. They do NOT apply mid-conversation to preserve prompt caching. Tell users this explicitly.\n\n5. **Cron jobs run in a fresh session** — Cron cron-run sessions have no current-chat context. Prompts must be fully self-contained. Skills and context_from are the only way to inject prior state.\n\n6. **Script-based cron jobs expect scripts in the profile's scripts/ directory** — Not `~/.hermes/scripts/`. The scheduler looks for scripts at `~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/scripts/<name>.sh`.\n\n7. **tmux sessions persist after Claude Code exits** — If Claude Code crashes or is killed mid-session, the tmux session remains. Always clean up with `tmux kill-session -t <name>` when done.\n\n8. **Gateway auth requires browser** — OAuth verification codes pasted via tmux `send-keys` fail because tmux treats them as password input. The user must log in on their local machine with a browser for initial auth.\n\n9. **Print mode can't write files** — Claude Code in `-p` mode refuses file writes. Use shell redirect (`> output.md 2>&1`) or the `write_file` tool instead.\n\n10. **Fable 5 session token budget** — Fable 5 has a per-session token budget. Running multiple parallel Fable 5 jobs can exhaust the budget mid-run. Run them sequentially or with max 2 concurrent.\n\n11. **Session resumption requires same directory** — `--continue` only works in the same working directory. For cross-directory work, capture the session ID and resume explicitly.\n\n12. **Context degradation is real** — AI output quality measurably degrades above 70% context window usage. Monitor with `/context` and proactively `/compact`.\n\n13. **`/exit` opens agent selector in OpenCode** — It's NOT a valid exit command. Use Ctrl+C to exit OpenCode sessions.\n\n14. **OpenCode needs `pty=true` for interactive mode** — OpenCode is a TUI app. Without PTY, it hangs. `opencode run` (bounded tasks) does NOT need PTY.\n\n15. **OAuth tokens are stored in `~/.hermes/auth.json`** — If auth is failing, check this file. Credential pools rotate automatically but can get stuck — use `hermes auth reset PROVIDER` to clear exhaustion.\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n```\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n```\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n```\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n```\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n```\n\n### Configuration\n\n```\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n```\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills uninstall N   Remove a hub skill\nhermes skills publish PATH  Publish to registry\nhermes skills browse        Browse all available skills\nhermes skills tap add REPO  Add a GitHub repo as skill source\n```\n\n### MCP Servers\n\n```\nhermes mcp serve            Run Hermes as an MCP server\nhermes mcp add NAME         Add an MCP server (--url or --command)\nhermes mcp remove NAME      Remove an MCP server\nhermes mcp list             List configured servers\nhermes mcp test NAME        Test connection\nhermes mcp configure NAME   Toggle tool selection\n```\n\n### Gateway (Messaging Platforms)\n\n```\nhermes gateway run          Start gateway foreground\nhermes gateway install      Install as background service\nhermes gateway start/stop   Control the service\nhermes gateway restart      Restart the service\nhermes gateway status       Check status\nhermes gateway setup        Configure platforms\n```\n\nSupported platforms: Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email, SMS, Matrix, Mattermost, Home Assistant, DingTalk, Feishu, WeCom, BlueBubbles (iMessage), Weixin (WeChat), API Server, Webhooks. Open WebUI connects via the API Server adapter.\n\nPlatform docs: https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/user-guide/messaging/\n\n### Sessions\n\n```\nhermes sessions list        List recent sessions\nhermes sessions browse      Interactive picker\nhermes sessions export OUT  Export to JSONL\nhermes sessions rename ID T Rename a session\nhermes sessions delete ID   Delete a session\nhermes sessions prune       Clean up old sessions (--older-than N days)\nhermes sessions stats       Session store statistics\n```\n\n### Cron Jobs\n\n```\nhermes cron list            List jobs (--all for disabled)\nhermes cron create SCHED    Create: '30m', 'every 2h', '0 9 * * *'\nhermes cron edit ID         Edit schedule, prompt, delivery\nhermes cron pause/resume ID Control job state\nhermes cron run ID          Trigger on next tick\nhermes cron remove ID       Delete a job\nhermes cron status          Scheduler status\n```\n\nFor script-only cron jobs, prefer `no_agent=true` and put the executable script or wrapper in the active profile's `scripts/` directory; the cron tool expects a relative script filename, not an absolute path. For backing up a live Hermes profile/home Git repository such as `~/.hermes`, verify the live repository path rather than creating a separate clone. See `references/profile-repo-hourly-git-sync.md`.\n\n### Webhooks\n\n```\nhermes webhook subscribe N  Create route at /webhooks/<name>\nhermes webhook list         List subscriptions\nhermes webhook remove NAME  Remove a subscription\nhermes webhook test NAME    Send a test POST\n```\n\n### Profiles\n\n```\nhermes profile list         List all profiles\nhermes profile create NAME  Create (--clone, --clone-all, --clone-from)\nhermes profile use NAME     Set sticky default\nhermes profile delete NAME  Delete a profile\nhermes profile show NAME    Show details\nhermes profile alias NAME   Manage wrapper scripts\nhermes profile rename A B   Rename a profile\nhermes profile export NAME  Export to tar.gz\nhermes profile import FILE  Import from archive\n```\n\nWhen syncing a profile `SOUL.md` from a GitHub agent identity file or installing companion skills from direct URLs, preserve exact source bytes and use non-interactive install flags (`--category`, `--yes`). See `references/profile-soul-sync-and-external-skill-install.md`.\n\n### Credential Pools\n\n```\nhermes auth add             Interactive credential wizard\nhermes auth list [PROVIDER] List pooled credentials\nhermes auth remove P INDEX  Remove by provider + index\nhermes auth reset PROVIDER  Clear exhaustion status\n```\n\n### Other\n\n```\nhermes insights [--days N]  Usage analytics\nhermes update               Update to latest version\nhermes pairing list/approve/revoke  DM authorization\nhermes plugins list/install/remove  Plugin management\nhermes honcho setup/status  Honcho memory integration (requires honcho plugin)\nhermes memory setup/status/off  Memory provider config\nhermes completion bash|zsh  Shell completions\nhermes acp                  ACP server (IDE integration)\nhermes claw migrate         Migrate from OpenClaw\nhermes uninstall            Uninstall Hermes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Slash Commands (In-Session)\n\nType these during an interactive chat session.\n\n### Session Control\n```\n/new (/reset)        Fresh session\n/clear               Clear screen + new session (CLI)\n/retry               Resend last message\n/undo                Remove last exchange\n/title [name]        Name the session\n/compress            Manually compress context\n/stop                Kill background processes\n/rollback [N]        Restore filesystem checkpoint\n/background <prompt> Run prompt in background\n/queue <prompt>      Queue for next turn\n/resume [name]       Resume a named session\n```\n\n### Configuration\n```\n/config              Show config (CLI)\n/model [name]        Show or change model\n/personality [name]  Set personality\n/reasoning [level]   Set reasoning (none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh|show|hide)\n/verbose             Cycle: off → new → all → verbose\n/voice [on|off|tts]  Voice mode\n/yolo                Toggle approval bypass\n/skin [name]         Change theme (CLI)\n/statusbar           Toggle status bar (CLI)\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n```\n/tools               Manage tools (CLI)\n/toolsets            List toolsets (CLI)\n/skills              Search/install skills (CLI)\n/skill <name>        Load a skill into session\n/cron                Manage cron jobs (CLI)\n/reload-mcp          Reload MCP servers\n/plugins             List plugins (CLI)\n```\n\n### Gateway\n```\n/approve             Approve a pending command (gateway)\n/deny                Deny a pending command (gateway)\n/restart             Restart gateway (gateway)\n/sethome             Set current chat as home channel (gateway)\n/update              Update Hermes to latest (gateway)\n/platforms (/gateway) Show platform connection status (gateway)\n```\n\n### Utility\n```\n/branch (/fork)      Branch the current session\n/fast                Toggle priority/fast processing\n/browser             Open CDP browser connection\n/history             Show conversation history (CLI)\n/save                Save conversation to file (CLI)\n/paste               Attach clipboard image (CLI)\n/image               Attach local image file (CLI)\n```\n\n### Info\n```\n/help

As of 2026-09-20 the\nMARION `fast`, `code`, `qwen3.6-35b-a3b`, and `qwen3.8-27b` entries are all configured at\n>=70K in the Hermes profile metadata; `frontier`/`deepseek-v4.1-flash-api` are provider-backed.\nIf a backend is raised above 64K but Hermes still aborts with a stale 32K value, update the\nprofile `custom_providers[].models[].context_length` metadata in addition to the server.\n\nWorkarounds when a model is still below floor:\n- Use only models whose backend reports ≥64K (today: qwen3.6-35b-a3b via .163/.164).\n- `fallback_providers` activation path does NOT re-check the floor → small models work as fallbacks.\n- Compressor auto-triggers at 85% of window for sub-floor models (safe).\n- Real fix: raise `--max-model-len` on .161 (qwen3.8, TP2/DP3) and .162 to ≥65536 via LLM Manager\n  hosting presets + service restart (memory: Qwen3.8 trains at 262K; 32K was a launch choice).\n\n## max_tokens pitfall\n\nCustom provider profile (`plugins/model-providers/custom/__init__.py`) sends\n`default_max_tokens=65536` when user hasn't set `model.max_tokens` → LiteLLM 400\n\"max_tokens=65536 cannot be greater than max_model_len=32768\". # Hermes Agent - Development Guide

Instructions for AI coding assistants and developers working on the hermes-agent codebase.

**Never give up on the right solution.**

## What Hermes Is

Hermes is a personal AI agent that runs the same agent core across a CLI, a
messaging gateway (Telegram, Discord, Slack, and ~20 other platforms), a TUI,
and an Electron desktop app. It learns across sessions (memory + skills),
delegates to subagents, runs scheduled jobs, and drives a real terminal and
browser. It is extended primarily through **plugins and skills**, not by
growing the core.

Two properties shape almost every design decision and are the lens for
reviewing any change:

- **Per-conversation prompt caching is sacred.** A long-lived conversation
  reuses a cached prefix every turn. Anything that mutates past context,
  swaps toolsets, or rebuilds the system prompt mid-conversation invalidates
  that cache and multiplies the user's cost. We do not do it (the one
  exception is context compression).
- **The core is a narrow waist; capability lives at the edges.** Every model
  tool we add is sent on every API call, so the bar for a new *core* tool is
  high. Most new capability should arrive as a CLI command + skill, a
  service-gated tool, or a plugin — not as core surface.

## Contribution Rubric — What We Want / What We Don't

This is the project's intent layer. Use it two ways:

1. **For humans and for your own work** — what gets merged and what gets
   rejected, so a contribution aims at the target.
2. **For automated review (the triage sweeper)** — guidance on when a PR is
   safe to close on the three allowed reasons (`implemented_on_main`,
   `cannot_reproduce`, `incoherent`) and, just as important, **when NOT to
   close** one. Taste-based "we don't want this / out of scope" closes are NOT
   an automated decision — those stay with a human maintainer. The sweeper's
   job here is to recognize design intent and *avoid wrongly closing a
   legitimate contribution*, not to make the won't-implement call itself.

Read the balance right: Hermes ships a **lot** — most merges are bug fixes to
real reported behavior, and the product surface (platforms, channels,
providers, models, desktop/TUI features) expands aggressively and on purpose.
The restraint below is aimed squarely at the **core agent + the model tool
schema**, the one place where every addition is paid for on every API call.
"Smallest footprint" governs *how a capability is wired into the core*, NOT
whether the product is allowed to grow. We are expansive at the edges and
conservative at the waist.

### What we want

- **Fix real bugs, well.** The bulk of what lands is `fix(...)` against an
  actual reported symptom. A good fix reproduces the symptom on current
  `main`, points to the exact line where it manifests, and fixes the whole bug
  class — sibling call paths included — not just the one site the reporter hit.
- **Expand reach at the edges.** New platform adapters, channels, providers,
  models, and desktop/TUI/dashboard features are welcome and land routinely,
  including large ones (a new messaging channel, a session-cap feature, a
  Windows PTY bridge). Breadth in the product is a goal, not a footprint
  concern — as long as it integrates with the existing setup/config UX
  (`hermes tools`, `hermes setup`, auto-install) rather than bolting on a raw
  env var.
- **Refactor god-files into clean modules.** Extracting a multi-thousand-line
  cluster out of `cli.py` / `run_agent.py` / `gateway/run.py` into a focused
  mixin or module is wanted work, even when the diff is huge and mechanical
  (large `+N/-N` refactors merge regularly). The "every line traces to the
  request" test applies to *feature* PRs; a declared refactor's request IS the
  extraction.
- **Keep the core narrow.** New *model tools* are the expensive exception —
  every tool ships on every API call. Prefer, in order: extend existing code →
  CLI command + skill → service-gated tool (`check_fn`) → plugin → MCP server
  in the catalog → new core tool (last resort). See "The Footprint Ladder."
- **Extend, don't duplicate.** Before adding a module/manager/hook, check
  whether existing infrastructure already covers the use case. When several PRs
  integrate the same *category*, design one shared interface instead of merging
  them one at a time (see the ABC + orchestrator note under the Footprint
  Ladder).
- **Behavior contracts over snapshots.** Tests should assert how two pieces of
  data must relate (invariants), not freeze a current value (model lists,
  config version literals, enumeration counts). See "Don't write
  change-detector tests."
- **E2E validation, not just green unit mocks.** For anything touching
  resolution chains, config propagation, security boundaries, remote
  backends, or file/network I/O, exercise the real path with real imports
  against a temp `HERMES_HOME`. Mocks hide integration bugs.
- **Cache-, alternation-, and invariant-safe.** Preserve prompt caching, strict
  message role alternation (never two same-role messages in a row; never a
  synthetic user message injected mid-loop), and a system prompt that is
  byte-stable for the life of a conversation.
- **Contributor credit preserved.** Salvage external work by cherry-picking
  (rebase-merge) so authorship survives in git history; don't reimplement from
  scratch when you can build on top.

### What we don't want (rejected even when well-built)

- **Speculative infrastructure.** Hooks, callbacks, or extension points with no
  concrete consumer. Adding a hook is easy; removing one after plugins depend
  on it is hard. A hook is NOT speculative if a contributor has a real, stated
  use case — even if the consumer ships separately.
- **New `HERMES_*` env vars for non-secret config.** `.env` is for secrets
  only (API keys, tokens, passwords). All behavioral settings — timeouts,
  thresholds, feature flags, display prefs — go in `config.yaml`. Bridge to an
  internal env var if the mechanism needs one, but user-facing docs point to
  `config.yaml`. Reject PRs that tell users to "set X in your .env" unless X
  is a credential.
- **A new core tool when terminal + file already do the job, or when a skill
  would.** If the only barrier is file visibility on a remote backend, fix the
  mount, not the toolset.
- **Lazy-reading escape hatches on instructional tools.** No `offset`/`limit`
  pagination on tools that load content the agent must read fully (skills,
  prompts, playbooks). Models will read page 1 and skip the rest.
- **"Fixes" that destroy the feature they secure.** A mitigation that kills the
  feature's purpose is the wrong mitigation. Read the original commit's intent
  (`git log -p -S`) before restricting behavior; find a fix that preserves the
  feature.
- **Outbound telemetry / usage attribution without opt-in gating.** No new
  analytics, third-party identifier tagging, or attribution tags until a
  generic user-facing opt-in (config gate + setup prompt + `hermes tools`
  toggle) exists. Park behind a label, do not merge.
- **Change-detector tests, cache-breaking mid-conversation, dead code wired in
  without E2E proof, and plugins that touch core files.** Plugins live in their
  own directory and work within the ABCs/hooks we provide; if a plugin needs
  more, widen the generic plugin surface, don't special-case it in core.
- **Third-party products / other people's projects integrated into the core
  tree.** Observability backends, vendor SaaS integrations, analytics dashboards,
  and similar "someone else's product" plugins do NOT land under `plugins/` in
  this repo. They place an ongoing maintenance burden on us to keep them working
  against a fast-moving core, for a backend we don't own. Ship them as a
  **standalone plugin repo** users install into `~/.hermes/plugins/` (or via a
  pip entry point), and promote them in the Nous Research Discord
  (`#plugins-skills-and-skins`). This is a coupling-and-maintenance decision, not

The number doesn't need to be exact —\n  221:     it gates the activate/skip decision, and a typical 200K context with a\n  222:     10% threshold means the decision flips around 20K tokens of schema.\n  223:     Order-of-magnitude precision is fine.\n  224:     \"\"\"\n  225:     total_chars = 0\n  226:     for td in tool_defs:\n  227:         try:\n  228:             total_chars += len(json.dumps(td, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(\",\", \":\")))\n  229:         except (TypeError, ValueError):\n  230:             total_chars += len(str(td))\n  231:     return int(math.ceil(total_chars / CHARS_PER_TOKEN))\n  232: \n  233: \n  234: def should_activate(\n  235:     config: ToolSearchConfig,\n  236:     deferrable_tokens: int,\n  237:     context_length: Optional[int],\n  238: ) -> bool:\n  239:     \"\"\"Decide whether tool search should activate for the current assembly.\n  240: \n  241:     ``\"off\"`` skips unconditionally. ``\"on\"`` activates unconditionally\n  242:     (as long as there is at least one deferrable tool — there's no point\n  243:     swapping a no-op). ``\"auto\"`` activates when the deferrable schemas\n  244:     would consume ``threshold_pct`` of context or more.\n  245:     \"\"\"\n  246:     if config.enabled == \"off\":\n  247:         return False\n  248:     if deferrable_tokens <= 0:\n  249:         return False\n  250:     if config.enabled == \"on\":\n  251:         return True\n  252:     # auto\n  253:     if not context_length or context_length <= 0:\n  254:         # Without a known context size, fall back to a fixed 20K-token cutoff\n  255:         # — the cliff above which Anthropic and OpenAI both saw quality drops.\n  256:         return deferrable_tokens >= 20_000\n  257:     threshold_tokens = int(context_length * (config.threshold_pct / 100.0))\n  258:     return deferrable_tokens >= threshold_tokens", "truncated": true}

{"output": "1675:def get_model_context_length(\n---\n        return None\n\n    # Extract the available_tokens figure.\n    # Anthropic format: \"… = available_tokens: 10000\"\n    patterns = [\n        r'available_tokens[:\\s]+(\\d+)',\n        r'available\\s+tokens[:\\s]+(\\d+)',\n        # fallback: last number after \"=\" in expressions like \"200000 - 190000 = 10000\"\n        r'=\\s*(\\d+)\\s*$',\n    ]\n    for pattern in patterns:\n        match = re.search(pattern, error_lower)\n        if match:\n            tokens = int(match.group(1))\n            if tokens >= 1:\n                return tokens\n\n    # OpenRouter/Nous format: \"maximum context length is N … (A of text input,\n    # B of tool input, C in the output)\". Available output = ctx - text - tool.\n    _m_ctx = re.search(r'maximum context length is (\\d+)', error_lower)\n    _m_parts = re.search(\n        r'\\((\\d+)\\s+of text input,\\s*(\\d+)\\s+of tool input,\\s*(\\d+)\\s+in the output\\)',\n        error_lower,\n    )\n    if _m_ctx and _m_parts:\n        _available = int(_m_ctx.group(1)) - int(_m_parts.group(1)) - int(_m_parts.group(2))\n        if _available >= 1:\n            return _available\n\n    # LM Studio / llama.cpp style: context window is reported in tokens but the\n    # prompt size is reported in CHARACTERS, e.g.\n    #   \"maximum context length is 65536 tokens ... your prompt contains 77409\n    #    characters ...\".\n    # Estimate the input tokens conservatively (~3 chars/token, which\n    # over-reserves the input so the retried output cap stays safely inside the\n    # window) and leave the remainder of the window for output.\n    _m_ctx_tok = re.search(r'maximum context length is (\\d+)\\s*token', error_lower)\n    _m_chars = re.search(r'prompt contains (\\d+)\\s*character', error_lower)\n    if _m_ctx_tok and _m_chars:\n        _ctx = int(_m_ctx_tok.group(1))\n        _est_input = (int(_m_chars.group(1)) + 2) // 3\n        _available = _ctx - _est_input\n        if _available >= 1:\n            return _available\n\n    return None\n\n\ndef _model_id_matches(candidate_id: str, lookup_model: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return True if *candidate_id* (from server) matches *lookup_model* (configured).\n\n    Supports two forms:\n    - Exact match:  \"nvidia-nemotron-super-49b-v1\" == \"nvidia-nemotron-super-49b-v1\"\n    - Slug match:   \"nvidia/nvidia-nemotron-super-49b-v1\" matches \"nvidia-nemotron-super-49b-v1\"\n                    (the part after the last \"/\" equals lookup_model)\n\n    This covers LM Studio's native API which stores models as \"publisher/slug\"\n    while users typically configure only the slug after the \"local:\" prefix.\n    \"\"\"\n    if candidate_id == lookup_model:\n        return True\n    # Slug match: basename of candidate equals the lookup name\n    if \"/\" in candidate_id and candidate_id.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[1] == lookup_model:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\ndef query_ollama_num_ctx(model: str, base_url: str, api_key: str = \"\") -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Query an Ollama server for the model's context length.\n\n    Returns the model's maximum context from GGUF metadata via ``/api/show``,\n    or the explicit ``num_ctx`` from the Modelfile if set.  Returns None if\n    the server is unreachable or not Ollama.\n\n    This is the value that should be passed as ``num_ctx`` in Ollama chat\n    requests to override the default 2048.\n    \"\"\"\n    import httpx\n\n    bare_model = _strip_provider_prefix(model)\n    server_url = base_url.rstrip(\"/\")\n    if server_url.endswith(\"/v1\"):\n        server_url = server_url[:-3]\n\n    try:\n        server_type = detect_local_server_type(base_url, api_key=api_key)\n    except Exception:\n        return None\n    if server_type != \"ollama\":\n        return None\n\n    headers = _auth_headers(api_key)\n\n    try:\n        with httpx.Client(timeout=3.0, headers=headers) as client:\n            resp = client.post(f\"{server_url}/api/show\", json={\"name\": bare_model})\n            if resp.status_code != 200:\n                return None\n            data = resp.json()\n\n            # Prefer explicit num_ctx from Modelfile parameters (user override)\n            params = data.get(\"parameters\", \"\")\n            if \"num_ctx\" in params:\n                for line in params.split(\"\\n\"):\n                    if \"num_ctx\" in line:\n                        parts = line.strip().split()\n                        if len(parts) >= 2:\n                            try:\n                                return int(parts[-1])\n                            except ValueError:\n                                pass\n\n            # Fall back to GGUF model_info context_length (training max)\n            model_info = data.get(\"model_info\", {})\n            for key, value in model_info.items():\n                if \"context_length\" in key and isinstance(value, (int, float)):\n                    return int(value)\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    return None", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

back to 128K despite the user having a per-model context_length set.\n1742|    # See #15779.\n1743|    if custom_providers and base_url and model:\n1744|        try:\n1745|            from hermes_cli.config import get_custom_provider_context_length\n1746|            cp_ctx = get_custom_provider_context_length(\n1747|                model=model,\n1748|                base_url=base_url,\n1749|                custom_providers=custom_providers,\n1750|            )\n1751|            if cp_ctx:\n1752|                return cp_ctx\n1753|        except Exception:\n1754|            pass  # {"output": "def classify_tools(tool_defs: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, Any]], List[Dict[str, Any]]]:\n    \"\"\"Split a tool-defs list into (visible, deferrable).\n\n    ``visible`` retains every tool that must stay in the model-facing array:\n    every core tool, plus any tool we can't classify. ``deferrable`` is the\n    candidate set for catalog entry.\n    \"\"\"\n    visible: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []\n    deferrable: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for td in tool_defs:\n        fn = td.get(\"function\") or {}\n        name = fn.get(\"name\", \"\")\n        if name in BRIDGE_TOOL_NAMES:\n            # Should never happen — bridge tools are added after classification —\n            # but be defensive.\n            continue\n        if is_deferrable_tool_name(name):\n            deferrable.append(td)\n        else:\n            visible.append(td)\n    return visible, deferrable\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Token estimation and threshold gate\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef estimate_tokens_from_schemas(tool_defs: Iterable[Dict[str, Any]]) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Estimate the token cost of a tool-defs list via the chars/4 rule.\n\n    Cheap and stable across providers. The number doesn't need to be exact —\n    it gates the activate/skip decision, and a typical 200K context with a\n    10% threshold means the decision flips around 20K tokens of schema.\n    Order-of-magnitude precision is fine.\n    \"\"\"\n    total_chars = 0\n    for td in tool_defs:\n        try:\n            total_chars += len(json.dumps(td, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(\",\", \":\")))\n        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n            total_chars += len(str(td))\n    return int(math.ceil(total_chars / CHARS_PER_TOKEN))\n\n\ndef should_activate(\n    config: ToolSearchConfig,\n    deferrable_tokens: int,\n    context_length: Optional[int],\n) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Decide whether tool search should activate for the current assembly.\n\n    ``\"off\"`` skips unconditionally. ``\"on\"`` activates unconditionally\n    (as long as there is at least one deferrable tool — there's no point\n    swapping a no-op). ``\"auto\"`` activates when the deferrable schemas\n    would consume ``threshold_pct`` of context or more.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "def is_deferrable_tool_name(name: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return True if a tool with this name is *eligible* for deferral.\n\n    A tool is deferrable iff it is registered with an MCP toolset prefix\n    OR it is not in ``_HERMES_CORE_TOOLS``. Core tools are never deferred\n    even when their toolset is technically plugin-provided (this protects\n    against accidental shadowing).\n    \"\"\"\n    if name in BRIDGE_TOOL_NAMES:\n        return False\n    if name in _core_tool_names():\n        return False\n    # Check registry toolset for MCP prefix.\n    try:\n        from tools.registry import registry\n        entry = registry.get_entry(name)\n        if entry is None:\n            return False\n        if entry.toolset.startswith(\"mcp-\"):\n            return True\n        # Non-MCP, non-core → plugin tool, eligible.\n        return True\n    except Exception:\n        return False\n\n\ndef classify_tools(tool_defs: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, Any]], List[Dict[str, Any]]]:\n    \"\"\"Split a tool-defs list into (visible, deferrable).\n\n    ``visible`` retains every tool that must stay in the model-facing array:\n    every core tool, plus any tool we can't classify. ``deferrable`` is the\n    candidate set for catalog entry.\n    \"\"\"\n    visible: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []\n    deferrable: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for td in tool_defs:\n        fn = td.get(\"function\") or {}\n        name = fn.get(\"name\", \"\")\n        if name in BRIDGE_TOOL_NAMES:\n            # Should never happen — bridge tools are added after classification —\n            # but be defensive.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

## ✅ Qwen3.8-27B Hermes 70K Context Failure — FIXED

**The plan's leading hypothesis (H1) was wrong.** This was **not** an illegal oversized `max_tokens` being misparsed. The backend 400 was *legitimate* — the input really was too big.

### Root cause (proven)
Hermes defers heavy MCP tools behind `tool_search`, but only when the deferrable set exceeds a threshold derived from the model's context window. That gate (`model_tools._resolve_active_context_length` → `get_model_context_length(model_id)`) calls the resolver **without `base_url`/`custom_providers`**, so the profile's `qwen3.8-27b: context_length: 70000` override was **ignored** — it resolved to **1,000,000**.

| model | ctx the gate resolved | 10% threshold | 91,481-tool MCP set |
|---|---|---|---|
| qwen3.6-35b-a3b | 262,144 | 26,214 | ✅ deferred → works |
| deepseek-v4.1-flash | 128,000 | 12,800 | ✅ deferred → works |
| **qwen3.8-27b** | **1,000,000** | **100,000** | ❌ **inlined** → overflow |

So every qwen3.8 request carried the full **159-tool / 91,481-token** MCP surface; with 16,384 reserved output the real 70,000-token window overflowed → `ContextWindowExceededError` → Hermes ran futile compression → `Cannot compress further` → **auto-reset**. qwen3.6 and DeepSeek resolved *lower*, deferred correctly, and worked — which is exactly why only qwen3.8 broke.

### Fix applied (config-only, no server change)
```yaml
tools:
  tool_search:
    enabled: 'on'      # was: auto
```
Forces unconditional deferral, independent of the mis-resolved context. No VM103 / LLM Manager change was needed — VM103 was already healthy at 70K. The running gateway picks this up on its next turn (config cache invalidates on mtime), so **no restart required**.

### Acceptance (real runs)
| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| Fresh session, trivial msg | ✅ `READY` |
| >10K request | ✅ `in=47,172` |
| Accumulated session + qwen3.8 + trivial msg (the reported shape) | ✅ `in=47,216`, **159 tools deferred**, no reset |
| 3 concurrent agents | ✅ 3× HTTP 200 |
| Regression: qwen3.6 / code / deepseek | ✅ all 200 |

### Still open (logged in `agents.md` + incident report)
- The upstream metadata defect persists (gate still mis-resolves). Config fix neutralises it; recommended upstream patch documented.
- The Zapier + read_ai MCP servers cost **91,481 tokens** — bigger than the entire conversation budget on a 70K backend. **Strongly recommend culling them for this profile** (biggest remaining lever).
- `max_tokens: 16384` eats 23% of the window; drop to 8192 if you want more conversation headroom.
- MCP servers are flapping (`stream disconnected` repeatedly) — separate issue.

Rollback is byte-exact: `cp config.yaml.pre-fix config.yaml`.

MEDIA:/home/jordatech/qwen38-hermes-fix-20260920-025503/QWEN38-HERMES-70K-CONTEXT-INCIDENT-20260919.md

Artifacts: `/home/jordatech/qwen38-hermes-fix-20260920-025503/` (config diff, backups, all logs, test scripts). Skill `hermes-local-model-setup` updated with the pitfall.

Want me to also **disable the unneeded MCP servers** for this profile (frees ~91K tokens) and/or **drop max_tokens to 8192**? Those are the two changes that turn "works" into "works with real 50K+ context."

# MARION-IA-USA Qwen3.8-27B Hermes Context Failure Fix Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-19  
**Priority:** High — Qwen3.8-27B currently cannot be used reliably by Hermes  
**Primary host:** MIAM-00111 / Proxmox VM103 / inference IP `10.0.20.161`  
**Control plane:** LLM Manager VM114 / `10.0.20.108`  
**Target model:** `cyankiwi/Qwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4`  
**Target served name:** `qwen3.8-27b`  
**Required Hermes context:** **70,000 tokens**  
**Desired concurrency:** 2–3 Hermes agents  
**Execution target:** 30–90 minutes; stop after 2 hours and preserve evidence if not fixed

---

## 1. Problem statement

The Telegram Hermes agent reports:

- `/model` successfully switches to `qwen3.8-27b`
- Provider is `marion`
- Hermes displays `Context: 70,000 tokens`
- A trivial message then immediately produces approximately:
  - `Context too large (~7,622 tokens) — compressing (1/3)...`
  - `Context length exceeded (...) Cannot compress further.`
  - `Session auto-reset`

DeepSeek and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B are working, so this task must focus narrowly on the Qwen3.8 request path and must **not destabilize the working models**.

The Qwen3.8 quantized model itself is not inherently limited to ~8K context. Its model config advertises `max_position_embeddings: 262144`. The intended MARION deployment intentionally caps it to **70,000** for Hermes and concurrency.

---

## 2. Leading hypothesis — test this FIRST

### H1 — Hermes is sending an illegal output-token cap to vLLM, then misclassifying the vLLM 400 as a prompt/context overflow

This symptom closely matches a current Hermes/vLLM bug family:

1. Hermes believes the context window is 70K.
2. The actual conversation is only ~7–8K tokens.
3. Hermes sends a request containing an excessive `max_tokens` / output cap.
4. vLLM rejects it because `input_tokens + max_tokens` cannot fit inside `max_model_len`.
5. Hermes classifies the vLLM 400 as a context overflow.
6. Hermes unnecessarily compresses the conversation.
7. The same illegal request is retried.
8. Hermes eventually says `Cannot compress further` and resets the session.

A public Hermes issue filed in September 2026 documents this exact failure mode with **Qwen3.8-27B INT4 on self-hosted vLLM**. The characteristic backend error is:

```text
max_tokens=... cannot be greater than max_model_len=max_total_tokens=...
Please request fewer output tokens
```

Do **not** assume this is the cause until the actual MARION logs show the same or equivalent response.

### Important distinction

`context_length` and `max_tokens` are not the same setting:

- `context_length = 70000` means the total request budget: system prompt + tool schemas + history + user input + generated output.
- `max_tokens` is only the maximum generated output for one response.

For Hermes agent work, keep the total context at **70,000**. Do **not** request 70,000 output tokens per turn.

---

## 3. Safety and rollback rules

Before changing anything:

1. Keep Qwen3.6 and DeepSeek deployments untouched.
2. Put only the MIAM-00111 / VM103 Qwen3.8 deployment into LLM Manager `MAINTENANCE` before restarting vLLM.
3. Do not reboot MIAM-00111.
4. Do not alter PCI passthrough, SR-IOV, RDMA, NIC, kernel, BIOS, or GPU binding.
5. Do not delete model caches.
6. Do not replace the known-good Qwen3.8 rollback preset.
7. Capture the current VM103 service and LLM Manager deployment before edits.
8. Back up the Hermes config/profile before changing it.
9. Prefer a configuration correction over a Hermes source patch.
10. If a Hermes source patch is required, make the smallest possible patch and save a diff/rollback copy.

Create a work directory such as:

```text
/home/jordatech/qwen38-hermes-fix-20260919-<HHMMSS>/
```

Save every diagnostic and changed file there.

---

## 4. Phase A — prove which layer is failing

**Timebox: 15–20 minutes. Do not make speculative changes before this phase is complete.**

There are four relevant layers:

```text
Hermes agent/profile
        ↓
LLM Manager / nginx / LiteLLM
        ↓
VM103 vLLM API
        ↓
Qwen3.8 model/runtime
```

We need to find the first layer that behaves incorrectly.

### A1. Capture Hermes version and active configuration

On the machine/container actually running `agent_stea004_entrepreneur`:

```bash
hermes --version 2>&1 || true
which hermes || true
python3 --version
```

Locate the active profile and gateway process rather than assuming paths:

```bash
ps auxww | grep -i '[h]ermes'
find ~/.hermes -maxdepth 4 -type f \( -name 'config.yaml' -o -name '*.yaml' -o -name '*.yml' \) -print 2>/dev/null
```

The existing environment has historically used a profile named:

```text
agent_stea004_entrepreneur
```

but verify the exact active profile/path from the running process.

Back up the relevant config before edits.

### A2. Inspect all context/output-token settings

Search active Hermes configuration and environment for:

```bash
grep -RniE 'context_length|max_tokens|max_output|compression|qwen3\.8|marion' ~/.hermes 2>/dev/null | head -300
env | grep -iE 'HERMES|MAX_TOKEN|CONTEXT' | sort
```

Record especially:

- `model.context_length`
- provider-specific `context_length`
- any `model.max_tokens`
- any `agent.max_tokens`
- any `HERMES_MAX_TOKENS`
- compression threshold settings
- the `marion` provider/model entry for `qwen3.8-27b`

Do not automatically equate an output cap with context length.

### A3. Capture the actual failing Hermes error

Reproduce once with a **new/fresh Hermes session** using `qwen3.8-27b` and a trivial prompt such as:

```text
Reply with only the word READY.
```

Immediately inspect Hermes gateway/agent logs. Search for:

```text
400
max_tokens
max_model_len
max_total_tokens
context length
context_overflow
compression
Cannot compress further
qwen3.8-27b
```

Example search:

```bash
grep -RniE 'max_tokens|max_model_len|max_total_tokens|cannot compress|context.*exceed|qwen3\.8' ~/.hermes/logs ~/.hermes 2>/dev/null | tail -300
```

**Highest-value evidence:** the raw HTTP/provider error returned by LLM Manager/vLLM before Hermes starts compressing.

### A4. Verify VM103 really serves 70K

From a machine that can reach VM103:

```bash
curl -sS http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1/models | python3 -m json.tool
```

Expected:

```text
id = qwen3.8-27b
max_model_len >= 70000
```

On VM103 also capture:

```bash
systemctl cat vllm
systemctl status vllm --no-pager
ps auxww | grep '[v]llm serve'
```

The actual running command must contain the intended:

```text
--max-model-len 70000
```

If direct `/v1/models` advertises less than 70K, skip to **Fix Path C** below.

### A5. Direct-backend short request

Send a tiny request directly to VM103 with a deliberately small output cap:

```json
{
  "model": "qwen3.8-27b",
  "messages": [{"role":"user","content":"Reply with READY only."}],
  "max_tokens": 64
}
```

Expected: HTTP 200.

If this fails, the problem is below LLM Manager/Hermes; investigate VM103/vLLM first.

### A6. Same request through LLM Manager

Send the same request using the normal LLM Manager API key and HTTPS endpoint, still with `max_tokens: 64`.

Expected: HTTP 200 routed to VM103.

If direct VM103 succeeds but LLM Manager fails, inspect the LLM Manager/LiteLLM request transformation before touching Hermes.

### A7. Inspect model metadata at both layers

Capture:

1. VM103 direct `/v1/models`
2. LLM Manager `/v1/models` using a valid agent key
3. LLM Manager registry row for `qwen3.8-27b`
4. LiteLLM model entry for `qwen3.8-27b`

Look specifically for accidental values such as:

```text
8192
32768
max_input_tokens
max_tokens
context_window
context_length
```

The intended value for the total model context is **70,000**.

---

## 5. Decision tree

Use the first matching path below.

---

# Fix Path A — oversized `max_tokens` / Hermes-vLLM parser bug

**Use this path if the raw error includes something equivalent to:**

```text
max_tokens=<N> cannot be greater than max_model_len=max_total_tokens=70000
```

or if a captured Hermes request clearly sends an absurdly large output budget.

This is the highest-probability path given the screenshot.

## A.1 Prefer removing the bad output-cap configuration

If the active Hermes version is sending a large `max_tokens` because one of these is explicitly configured:

```text
model.max_tokens
agent.max_tokens
HERMES_MAX_TOKENS
```

then remove/disable that oversized global setting **if it is not required by the working models**.

The goal is:

```text
context_length = 70000
output max_tokens = omitted OR a modest output cap
```

For a temporary targeted diagnostic, an output cap of **4096 or 8192** is reasonable. Do not confuse this with the 70K conversation context.

Restart only the Hermes gateway/process required to reload the config. Do not restart VM103 merely for a Hermes config change.

Re-test a fresh Qwen3.8 session.

### A.2 If current Hermes ignores configuration and still triggers the bug

Do **not** blindly upgrade the entire Hermes installation first.

Capture:

- exact Hermes version/commit
- whether the upstream vLLM error-parser fix is included
- local modifications

As of 2026-09-19, the public issue/fix for the specific vLLM error wording is still represented by open Hermes issue/PR work. If the installed Hermes build does not contain equivalent logic, apply the smallest local patch modeled on the upstream fix.

Patch only the error classification/parsing path so Hermes recognizes vLLM output-cap errors such as:

```text
max_model_len=max_total_tokens=70000
```

The patch needs to ensure:

1. the response is classified as an output-cap problem rather than prompt-too-long;
2. the model/context limit can be parsed from vLLM's error wording;
3. Hermes retries with a legal output cap rather than repeatedly compressing the prompt.

Before patching:

```bash
cp -a <hermes-source-file> <hermes-source-file>.pre-qwen38-fix-20260919
```

After patching, save:

```bash
git diff > qwen38-hermes-vllm-output-cap-fix.diff
```

Run any focused unit tests available for model metadata/output-cap parsing.

Then restart only Hermes/gateway and retest.

### A.3 Do not solve this by inflating VM103 context

Do not increase VM103 from 70K to 262K merely to hide an illegal Hermes output cap. That would consume more KV-cache capacity and work against the requirement for 2–3 concurrent coding agents.

---

# Fix Path B — Hermes stale/incorrect context metadata

Use this if:

- VM103 direct endpoint is genuinely 70K,
- LLM Manager is correct,
- no oversized `max_tokens` error exists,
- but Hermes internally behaves as though Qwen3.8 is 8K/16K/32K.

## B.1 Confirm what Hermes actually resolved

Use `/usage`, model-info output, startup logs, or equivalent to capture the resolved model window.

The `/model` UI displaying 70,000 is useful but not sufficient; confirm the actual runtime agent object/provider metadata also uses 70,000.

## B.2 Make the provider/model context explicit

For the MARION custom provider, explicitly set the Qwen3.8 entry to:

```yaml
context_length: 70000
```

Do not add a 70,000 `max_tokens` output setting.

If Hermes has a persistent discovered-context cache, remove/refresh **only the Qwen3.8/marion cache entry**, not unrelated model/provider state.

Restart the Hermes gateway and verify `/usage`/runtime metadata again.

---

# Fix Path C — VM103 is not actually serving 70K

Use this if direct:

```text
http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1/models
```

reports a lower `max_model_len` or the running vLLM command does not contain the intended 70K configuration.

## C.1 Preserve current service

Save:

```bash
systemctl cat vllm
systemctl status vllm --no-pager
ps auxww | grep '[v]llm serve'
```

Confirm the LLM Manager rollback preset exists.

## C.2 Restore the intended 70K production preset

Preferred Qwen3.8 configuration remains:

```text
model: cyankiwi/Qwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4
served model: qwen3.8-27b
max model len: 70000
TP: 2
DP: 3
PP: 1
EP: not applicable
prefix caching: enabled
chunked prefill: enabled
```

Use TP2/DP3 if it fits reliably. If 70K KV pressure prevents stable operation, use the already-planned TP3/DP2 fallback for two agents.

Do not reduce the context below 70K.

Restart only `vllm.service`, wait for `/v1/models`, and verify `max_model_len >= 70000` before returning LLM Manager desired state to `SERVING`.

---

# Fix Path D — LLM Manager/LiteLLM is mutating or mis-advertising Qwen3.8

Use this if:

- direct VM103 request succeeds,
- direct VM103 metadata is 70K,
- but the same request through LLM Manager fails or reports incorrect model metadata.

Inspect:

- model registry row
- alias `code`
- LiteLLM model configuration
- any `max_tokens`, `max_input_tokens`, `context_window`, or model-info overrides
- nginx routing only to confirm the expected request path

Correct the Qwen3.8 entry so LLM Manager forwards the request without inventing an output token budget and advertises/records the 70K total context accurately.

Do not alter the working `fast`/Qwen3.6 or DeepSeek entries.

---

## 6. Required acceptance tests after the fix

Do not call the issue fixed just because `/model` displays `70,000`.

### Test 1 — trivial fresh-session request

In a fresh Telegram/Hermes session:

1. select `qwen3.8-27b`;
2. verify it displays provider `marion` and 70,000 context;
3. send `Hello` or a one-second voice message;
4. receive a normal model response;
5. **no compression warning**;
6. **no auto-reset**.

This directly reproduces the previously failing behavior.

### Test 2 — tool-call smoke test

Ask Qwen3.8/Hermes to perform one benign tool call available to the agent.

Pass criteria:

- valid tool call
- tool result returned
- model continues afterward
- no context reset

### Test 3 — 10K–15K request/session

Build a controlled conversation/request above the ~7.6K point that previously failed.

Pass criteria:

- no false context-overflow recovery
- no `Cannot compress further`
- no reset

### Test 4 — 50K–60K context

Use a synthetic long-context request or accumulated session that reaches at least **50K tokens**, ideally ~60K, but remains under the 70K total budget with room for output.

Pass criteria:

- request accepted
- model returns useful output
- no vLLM OOM
- no Hermes false reset

Do not request 70K input plus a large output; total input + output must remain under the server context ceiling.

### Test 5 — concurrent coding-agent target

After single-agent correctness is proven:

- run 2 simultaneous Hermes agents through LLM Manager;
- if stable, run 3 simultaneous agents;
- use realistic moderate context first, then one long-context load test.

Minimum acceptable result: **2 concurrent agents**.  
Target result: **3 concurrent agents**.

Capture TTFT and obvious throughput degradation, but do not launch a long benchmark campaign.

---

## 7. LLM Manager cleanup after success

When accepted:

1. Save the fixed deployment as or update the preset:

```text
HERMES-QWEN38-70K-CODE-PRODUCTION
```

2. Preserve prior Qwen3.8 presets as rollback profiles.
3. Ensure `code` points to the fixed deployment.
4. Restore VM103 desired state to `SERVING`.
5. Ensure the registry has no stale healthy/routable Qwen3.8 row pointing to an old 25K/32K deployment.
6. Record the actual accepted:
   - vLLM command
   - TP/DP layout
   - context length
   - max-num-seqs
   - KV/cache configuration
   - Hermes version/commit
   - Hermes context configuration
   - whether a local Hermes patch was required
7. Save a concise incident note explaining the failure and fix.

---

## 8. Required final incident report

Write:

```text
QWEN38-HERMES-70K-CONTEXT-INCIDENT-20260919.md
```

It must contain:

### Root cause

State the proven cause, not the initial hypothesis.

### Evidence

Include:

- raw Hermes/provider error
- direct VM103 `/v1/models`
- manager `/v1/models`
- relevant Hermes config
- vLLM running command

### Fix

List exact files/settings changed.

### Rollback

Give exact rollback steps.

### Acceptance

Record PASS/FAIL for:

- fresh trivial Hermes request
- tool call
- >10K context
- 50K–60K context
- two concurrent agents
- three concurrent agents

### Remaining risks

Examples:

- local Hermes source patch may be overwritten by `hermes update`
- open upstream bug not yet merged/released
- 70K context reduces concurrency relative to 32K

---

## 9. Stop conditions

Stop and preserve evidence instead of making broad changes if any of these occur:

- Qwen3.6 or DeepSeek becomes unhealthy
- VM103 loses GPUs
- PCI/VFIO/SR-IOV errors appear
- a proposed fix requires Proxmox host reboot
- a proposed fix requires changing the working LLM Manager API-key architecture
- a Hermes full-version upgrade would overwrite unknown local modifications
- the issue cannot be isolated within 2 hours

If blocked, restore the previous VM103 preset and leave `code` disabled/unhealthy rather than advertising a broken model.

---

## 10. Recommended execution order

```text
0–10 min   Capture baseline + backups
10–20 min  Reproduce once and capture raw provider/vLLM error
20–30 min  Direct VM103 vs LLM Manager comparison
30–45 min  Apply the matching fix path
45–60 min  Fresh-session + >10K Hermes acceptance
60–90 min  50K–60K test + 2/3-agent concurrency + documentation
```

The task should not require changing model weights or re-downloading Qwen3.8 unless diagnostics prove the current model files are invalid.

---

## 11. Most likely successful outcome

Based on the observed screenshot, the highest-probability successful outcome is:

```text
VM103 Qwen3.8 itself is healthy at 70K.
LLM Manager routing is healthy.
Hermes sends or interprets an output-token limit incorrectly for the vLLM backend.
vLLM returns a 400 output-cap error.
Hermes mistakes that response for prompt/context overflow and enters futile compression.
Correcting/removing the oversized output cap or applying the narrow vLLM error-parser fix stops the false compression/reset loop.
```

Do not modify Qwen3.8 tensor/data parallelism unless the direct backend tests show an actual vLLM/context-capacity problem.

## ✅ Qwen3.8-27B Hermes 70K Context Failure — FIXED

**The plan's leading hypothesis (H1) was wrong.** This was **not** an illegal oversized `max_tokens` being misparsed. The backend 400 was *legitimate* — the input really was too big.

### Root cause (proven)
Hermes defers heavy MCP tools behind `tool_search`, but only when the deferrable set exceeds a threshold derived from the model's context window. That gate (`model_tools._resolve_active_context_length` → `get_model_context_length(model_id)`) calls the resolver **without `base_url`/`custom_providers`**, so the profile's `qwen3.8-27b: context_length: 70000` override was **ignored** — it resolved to **1,000,000**.

| model | ctx the gate resolved | 10% threshold | 91,481-tool MCP set |
|---|---|---|---|
| qwen3.6-35b-a3b | 262,144 | 26,214 | ✅ deferred → works |
| deepseek-v4.1-flash | 128,000 | 12,800 | ✅ deferred → works |
| **qwen3.8-27b** | **1,000,000** | **100,000** | ❌ **inlined** → overflow |

So every qwen3.8 request carried the full **159-tool / 91,481-token** MCP surface; with 16,384 reserved output the real 70,000-token window overflowed → `ContextWindowExceededError` → Hermes ran futile compression → `Cannot compress further` → **auto-reset**. qwen3.6 and DeepSeek resolved *lower*, deferred correctly, and worked — which is exactly why only qwen3.8 broke.

### Fix applied (config-only, no server change)
```yaml
tools:
  tool_search:
    enabled: 'on'      # was: auto
```
Forces unconditional deferral, independent of the mis-resolved context. No VM103 / LLM Manager change was needed — VM103 was already healthy at 70K. The running gateway picks this up on its next turn (config cache invalidates on mtime), so **no restart required**.

### Acceptance (real runs)
| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| Fresh session, trivial msg | ✅ `READY` |
| >10K request | ✅ `in=47,172` |
| Accumulated session + qwen3.8 + trivial msg (the reported shape) | ✅ `in=47,216`, **159 tools deferred**, no reset |
| 3 concurrent agents | ✅ 3× HTTP 200 |
| Regression: qwen3.6 / code / deepseek | ✅ all 200 |

### Still open (logged in `agents.md` + incident report)
- The upstream metadata defect persists (gate still mis-resolves). Config fix neutralises it; recommended upstream patch documented.
- The Zapier + read_ai MCP servers cost **91,481 tokens** — bigger than the entire conversation budget on a 70K backend. **Strongly recommend culling them for this profile** (biggest remaining lever).
- `max_tokens: 16384` eats 23% of the window; drop to 8192 if you want more conversation headroom.
- MCP servers are flapping (`stream disconnected` repeatedly) — separate issue.

Rollback is byte-exact: `cp config.yaml.pre-fix config.yaml`.
